   "branding": "Logo appears only on the pack (vertical lowercase serif). One accent, lemon yellow (~#FFD400), is used twice (arc and proof box), which ties the headline to the proof.    "copy": "6-word headline, 'Oil-free till the sun's gone': a benefit plus a duration measured by the sun rather than in hours. The 10AM/5PM endpoints work as data labels. The descriptor names texture, category and skin type. Proof is a 2-word lab chip. Claim type: duration.",

 "patterns": {
  "visual": "Every winner makes one claim literal with one prop or setting: ice for cool, a spotlight for evening, a light-burst for bold, a ladder for steps, a splash or explosion for ingredients, a pour for texture, a neon frame for a shop window, a sun-arc for duration. Colour worlds come in two kinds: the pack colour pushed tone-on-tone (Foxtale 22/28/52/53/54), or one saturated flat field (BSC cyan, Renee magenta). What Naira lacks, judged against its documented volumes (docs/creative/README.md, keep-it-on/README.md): it has shot craft macros and wet bodies, but no frame where a graphic layer (an arc, leader lines, numerals, swap arrows) explains the claim, no flat saturated colour field, no product caught mid-motion, and no product cast as a personality. The piece names in this set come from /home/user/naira-scroll/docs/creative/stock-2nov/build/listings.json (2 Nov). Check live stock (20 or more) and the live price before building any of them.",
  "camera": "Product-only CGI or packshot dominates (15 of 19). The product is scaled up to 45-90% of the frame height, usually tilted 5-40° and cropped off one or two edges. A hard key or backlight gives a star glint or bloom, and low or eye-level angles make the product monumental. Bodies appear only as fragments (lips, forearm, chin-to-chest, fingertips) and always serve a demonstration (11, 22, 24, 40). For Naira: a macro product 'monument' is fine with a true-size mm tag; any body shot is generated in Higgsfield at true size; forced perspective only with the fingers in the same plane as the piece. Rank 22's oversized bottle on an arm is the one camera move that cannot transfer.",
  "layout": "1:1 with a hard two-zone split: product on one side, text on the other. Right column or lower-right in 1/22/28/52/53/54; top band in 3/17/26/27/40/55/56; left column in 11/15/24/42. The headline is the largest type unless a number is (11, 22). Text takes 10-40% of the frame. Supporting lines are hairline leader callouts (40, 55) or numbered and verb triads (22, 28, 52). None uses a CTA button. None shows a price; only one shows savings (24), in a bottom band with legal microtext. Logos sit only on the pack; there are no corner logos.",
  "branding": "The pack carries the brand, plus one accent colour lifted from it into a headline keyword or proof chip (Foxtale yellow/orange, Sugar pink, BSC cyan, mCaffeine blue). One display face plus one sans, with italic or a colour change on the second beat (4, 40, 55). Series consistency shows: BSC 26/42 share font and blue, and Foxtale 53/54 share the same right-side text slot. Naira's equivalent: Oxblood #6E1A20, Near-Black #3A0F13, Bone #F2EDE4 and Warm Gold #C2A05A, with the accent on the second beat. No corner logo is needed if a box, card or tag carries the name.",
  "copy": "Short two-beat structures dominate: 'Bold X. Zero Y.' (4), 'Stays put. Steals attention.' (55), 'No drama. Just freshness.' (26), 'Tough on X / gentle on Y' (17). The others are number-first (72 Hrs, 32%), verb triads (Hydrate/Nourish/Repair; Exfoliates/Brightens/Clears; Reveal/Repair/Correct), simile ('Glow like you just left a Spa'), personification ('The chill one'), imperative pairs ('Kohl it! Wing it!') and rule plus escape clause ('Spray it in the evening / Or whenever...'). Claim types: duration (1, 11, 40, 53, 55), formula or spec (15, 22, 53, 54), value maths (24). Proof sits beside the claim as a chip, a stat card or a footnote. OWNER DECISION: duration is the claim this group is built on ('all day', '72 hours', 'till the sun's gone', 'stays put'). Naira's strongest matching truth is the water and colour-fade line in its listings' Care blocks (per keep-it-on/README.md), which the house rule bans. Within the rule, duration can only be told as hours of wear (9 AM to 2 AM) and as material (316L, PVD, 18k gold tone) where the listing says so."
 },
 "devices": [
  "Sun-arc time scale over the product: a drawn arc with start and end times at its feet and the headline set along the curve (1)",
  "Single hard spotlight on black: one lit object on a white round riser, visible lamp bloom, long shadow toward camera, ~90% black frame (3)",
  "Light-burst set: white light blades radiating from behind a product caught mid-swing with a ribbon of its own material (4)",
  "Giant-number headline, plus a frosted proof card holding one verified fact with a footnote (11)",
  "Heroic plinth against sky: low angle, hard sun flare, product shown twice (closed/open or front/back) (15)",
  "Antithesis line 'Tough on X / gentle on Y' with the product exploding out of its own materials (17)",
  "Giant hairline numeral in the accent colour, plus three VERB + object bullets in a right column (22)",
  "Fingertip push-to-lens, with an angled offer band at the bottom carrying live terms in microtext (24)",
  "Monumental crop: a packshot tilted ~20° bleeding off two edges on a flat graphic field with shapes clipped at the edges (26, 42)",
  "'No X. Just Y.' negation two-beat that runs on into the body copy (26)",
  "Simile headline '___ like you just left ___' in serif over a centred packshot with material props (27)",
  "Ladder or step prop, with hairline numerals 1-2-3, each with a verb and proof (28)",
  "Split-mode V with curved swap arrows and two 'Verb it!' script labels (34)",
  "Full-bleed body macro with dot-ended hairline leader callouts and no packshot (40)",
  "Personality naming, 'The ___ one.', with each variant colour-coded as a series (42)",
  "Tipped container pouring its contents into its own lid, plus a stacked verb triad (52)",
  "Tone-on-tone gradient float in the product's own colour, with text right-centre (53)",
  "Product encased in a clear shape, with an exploded layer or molecule diagram (54)",
  "Product splits the headline: a vertical object cuts the type in two and leaves its trace below (ink line, chain coil) (55)",
  "Glowing neon rectangle as a shop-window frame, with sensory props scattered around it and a typewriter headline (56)",
  "Second-beat accent: the second phrase of a two-beat headline switches to italic and the brand accent colour (4, 40, 55)",
  "Naira honesty add-on: a true-size mm/cm tag from the listing on every macro packshot, so a monument crop never oversells size"
 ],
 "best_three": [

 ]

 "patterns": {
  "visual": "Two mechanisms carry this group. (1) The product plus its trace, the mark it leaves: lip swatch (67), lash stroke (77), mist (66), drip (108), splash (109), shimmer swoosh (68), swatch stripes (120), smear (99). (2) A one-object metaphor: glass shield (87), cream heart (78), padlock pack (82), cutaway 'hug' (79), levitation (96). Every frame lives in one colour world sampled from the pack: blue (57), red (67), yellow (87), lilac (99), pink (69, 109, 115), grey (66, 95). What Naira is missing: our frames show the piece but never its trace (its light, the line of the chain, its stone colour as a field) or a metaphor object, and our grounds are beige rather than sampled from the piece.",
  "camera": "Frontal or 3/4 packshots on seamless grounds. The hero is shown huge and bleeds off one or two edges (57, 66, 67, 87, 95, 107). Flat lays use top-left light on a diagonal (77, 78). Objects float with a detached shadow (95, 96, 82). Touch enters from a frame edge mid-action: a pinch (57), a press (66), a squeeze on skin (86). Light is soft everywhere except the hard-shadow monochrome (77) and the golden 3D render (82). Only 1 of 19 shows a face. For Naira, a product-only macro is the honest way to make a small piece huge. Worn shots come only from Higgsfield, at true size.",
  "layout": "Three grids. Half-split, product left and type right (57, 66, 68, 77, 87, 107, 108). Centred headline over the product (69, 79, 115, 120). Bottom-left type under a cropped product (67, 86, 95). Type takes 10–35% of the frame. Headlines run to two lines with a weight jump (light, then black, as in 68, 77, 96). There is no CTA button on any of the 19 and no price on any of them. Proof sits in a disc, seal, tag or callout instead. What Naira is missing: our cards spend their space on price chips and lockups; these spend it on one claim and one proof object.",
  "branding": "Pack-as-logo: the brand name is printed on the hero object. The type colour is sampled from the pack. A separate logo is rare and small: top-right (68), a campaign lockup (57), a bottom sign-off band (107). Series signals include a house glyph (the sparkle in 68), a fixed sign-off band (107), and 3D type rendered in the product's material (69, 115). What Naira is missing: jewellery carries no pack-logo, so we need one constant sign-off device (a band or tab in Naira rose) and a type colour sampled from the piece on every card.",
  "copy": "Headlines run 2 to 11 words. The devices: antithesis 'X. Not Y.' naming the pain (57); couplet 'Looks like X. Feels like Y.' (79); 'X on A, Y on B' (96); slang twist 'Your X, But Lit.' (68); a pun on the pack's form or the product name (82, 115, 77); an absolute two-word claim (95); a sensory triplet (67); a verb ladder (66); the ingredient as hero (78, 99, 107); a number as hero (87, 99, 108, 109); a Q&A label (109). Proof is always a number or a third-party mark. OWNER DECISION: 7 of 19 lean on durability, water or safety claims (66, 77, 82, 86, 87, 95, 120). Under the house rules Naira must swap these for price, material (18k gold tone, cubic zirconia, steel where listed), mechanism (toggle, hinge, adjustable where listed) and wit. A waterproof or tarnish-free claim would make the 57, 66, 77, 86, 87, 95 and 120 versions noticeably stronger, if the owner ever verifies and allows it."
 },
 "devices": [
  "Antithesis headline 'GOLD TONE SHINE. NOT GOLD PRICES.': two short sentences, the second negating the category pain, in heavy condensed white caps (57)",
  "Frame-break: the hero overlaps a saturated inset panel and spills onto its white margin (57)",
  "Proof disc straddling the panel edge, carrying exactly one verified number (price, stone count, code) (57, 99)",
  "Verb ladder: three huge right-aligned single verbs, each with a 2-line small-caps qualifier with one bold word (66)",
  "Frozen action: a fingertip mid-press or mid-clasp, with a light streak or chain arc carrying the eye to the type (66, 109)",
  "Colour-block horizon: top half neutral, bottom half a thick paint slab mixed to the stone colour, the piece entering from the top edge on the centre axis and touching the horizon (67)",
  "'Your [ordinary garment], But Lit.' slang twist, with the piece laid on that garment at true scale (68)",
  "3D glossy headline type rendered in the product's material or stone colour (69, 115)",
  "Rising plinths: three colourways on clear cylinders of stepped height, backlit so the stones glow (69)",
  "Swatch-as-diagram: one black ink line drawing beside the product showing its effect or fit (an ear curve continuing the huggie arc) (77)",
  "Black authority disc top-right, filled only with a verified fact (free insured shipping, COD) (77)",
  "Material-drawn shape: a chain laid as a heart that becomes the text panel (78)",
  "Cutaway plus hairline leader-line callouts with dot ends, under the 'Looks like X. Priced/Feels like Y.' couplet (79)",
  "Form pun with a sticker: the piece's own shape gives the joke ('KNOT BASIC'), over an illustrated Indian cityscape with a bubble-letter offer sticker (82)",
  "Bracketed tag stack: two translucent tag boxes joined by a thin vertical rule, under a two-colour caps headline (86)",
  "Tone-on-tone monochrome world sampled from the piece, with one symbolic glass object behind it (87, 99)",
  "Abstract crop: the piece rotated 35° and bleeding off two edges, floating, with a two-word 'IT'S ___.' claim in the bottom 30% (95)",
  "Levitation: the piece hovering with its shadow detached far below; the type size steps down line by line (96)",
  "Inside-the-box reveal at a 35° high angle, plus a constant full-width brand sign-off band at the foot of every card (107)",
  "Spill/drip: the chain pouring over the edge of an open box like product oozing from a nozzle; tone chip pills as variant selectors (108)",
  "Dashed 'WHAT IS IT?' label answering the scroller's first question, as card 1 of a Q&A carousel (109)",
  "Range-as-stripes: colourway bracelets laid as parallel diagonals slicing across the whole frame (120)",
  "Mixed-weight two-line headline: line 1 regular, line 2 extra-bold, in a colour sampled from the product (68, 77, 96)"
 ],

 ]

 "patterns": {
  "visual": "Two families. (1) A packshot plus its ingredient or texture as a prop: gel discs (122), swatch swoosh (149), gel swirl (157, 180), coffee beans (130), rice mound (187), rosemary, rose, tropical fruit. (2) The product placed in the world its benefit lives in: bedroom (155), stage (166), beach (163), blazing sun (175), lab (138). The strongest outliers drop the packshot habit: no product at all (169), an extreme part crop (199), the feature in action (197 panel open, 128 hands opening the jar). Each ad has one colour world, lifted from the product or ingredient. Gap for Naira: jewellery has no 'ingredient', so we need material stand-ins (loose CZ stones, satin ribbon, glass lenses, jasmine, velvet, brass urli) and a single colour world per ad.",
  "camera": "Mostly front-on cut-outs at eye level in soft high-key CGI light, with the product at 65-90% of height on one half of the frame. Energy comes from three moves: a 20-30 degree diagonal tilt (122, 123, 160); hard directional light, such as window sun with long shadows (123), a light beam across a gradient (160), a rim light (130) or a sun flare (175); and very shallow depth of field when a person or set sits behind (155, 166). Low hero angles turn objects into monuments (175, 187). Scale stays honest: props sit at true relative size, and the only hands (128) are at true size. For jewellery, the low-angle 'monument' read comes from macro on pebble-scale sets, never from compositing.",
  "layout": "Split screen dominates: product on one half, type on the other (about 12 of 19). The headline sits top-left or in the right column, 2-4 lines, mixing light and heavy weights. Support comes from check bullets or icon rows (130, 149, 160, 163, 175), one roundel seal in a corner (138, 149, 160, 187, 188), and a full-width footnote under any number (138, 157, 187). Type covers 12-40% of the frame (169 is ~60%). Almost none carry a price, CTA, offer or brand lockup, because they are product-page gallery cards. The Naira ad version needs one small offer/price strip and a logo in the same bottom safe zone every time.",
  "branding": "The logo lives on the pack, not the canvas. Headline colour is sampled from the product or background (tonal type: 122 brown, 130 coffee, 138 slate, 149 taupe). Each brand runs one series system: Pilgrim is teal serif + seal + footnote; Plum is navy condensed + yellow sparkles. Seals and roundels work as brand stamps. Naira equivalent: a sage roundel for the offer/fact, Anton (heavy) + Cormorant italic (serif) + Jost (body), and a small sage logo in a fixed position, because the jewellery itself carries no printed logo.",
  "copy": "Headlines run 2-7 words of benefit, rarely a sentence. Formulas: adjective + noun ('Crystal clear', 'Luxurious shine'), 'X meets Y', 'Stop X. Start Y.', 'More X', 'Like a pro', 'Your daily X, now Y', stat + speed ('95% after 1 application', 'in 1 wash'), equation ('40% = beastly longevity'), and a named feature with character ('ninja wheels') plus a sensory simile. Typographic jokes: a toggle as the O in UNLOCK, a sparkle on the i in SHiNE. Proof comes from numbers with asterisks and footnotes, 'tested' seals and borrowed event authority. Objections are removed with 'No ___' / 'without ___'. For Naira, proof must be listing facts and offers: gold tone, CZ, steel/PVD where listed, NAIRA10/BUY2/3/4, free insured shipping, COD. The stat + speed and 'No ___' formulas are the ones that most invite banned claims (owner decisions flagged on 160 and 175)."
 },
 "devices": [
  "In-word object swap: replace a letter with the product part (toggle ring as the O in 'TOGGLE ON', a CZ sparkle as the dot on the i in 'SHiNE')",
  "Equation stack on dark stone: question plate on top, giant number, '=', outcome; no product or a tiny packshot at the foot ('3 COILS = A WHOLE STACK IN ONE BANGLE')",
  "'Stop X. Start Y.' imperative couplet on a full-width colour band, with the hand or lid breaking out of the band for depth",
  "Escalating headline: the last line set biggest so the speed or result is the loudest word ('Wedding-guest ready / IN 1 CLASP')",
  "'X meets Y' mash-up headline with props that literally show both X and Y at true relative scale",
  "Tonal texture field: the background is the product's material blown up (clear lens discs for CZ, a satin ribbon swoosh for gold tone), and the headline colour is sampled from it",
  "Destination circle: a full-bleed location photo plus a circular vignette of the piece in a prop vessel, with a flower breaking the circle edge",
  "Stage curtain: an oxblood velvet curtain, a terracotta ledge and a spotlight from upper left, falling off to black",
  "Enemy backdrop: the piece as a monument against golden-hour sun flare, shot macro on pebbles so they read as cliffs",
  "Corner roundel stamp: one circle top-right carrying a true offer or fact (BUY2, COD, FREE INSURED SHIPPING) in place of borrowed authority",
  "Asterisk + full-width footnote used honestly to state code terms or the listing source of a spec",
  "Feature-reveal crop: the moving part (clasp, hinge, box lid) swung toward the lens at ~30 degrees, with the object bleeding off two edges",
  "Extreme part-crop with a single saturated accent plus a personified feature name and a sensory simile ('Whisper-click huggies')",
  "Diagonal rhythm: 3-6 units on parallel diagonals under hard window sun, with long shadows and units cropping off every edge",
  "Bedside/dresser ritual: the piece sharp in the foreground, the wearer blurred behind in the moment of use (a true-size generated worn shot)",
  "'No ___' check list for objection removal, limited to allowed truths ('No sizing guesswork', 'No delivery fee')"
 ],
 "best_three": [
  155,
  128,
  169
 ]
}

   "visual_idea": "The named ingredient becomes a co-star: a halved papaya, seeds facing camera, acts as a cushion the tube leans on. The orange ground is taken from the tube's orange band, so ground, pack and fruit flesh form one colour family. The fruit says 'papaya formula' with no words.",
   "camera": "Straight-on, eye level, normal lens, tight crop. The tube enters from bottom-left at about 25 degrees and is cut off by the bottom and left edges, so it reads bigger than the frame. Everything is sharp; there is no depth-of-field play. Product plus prop take about 55% of the frame, all in the left half.",
   "layout": "Square 1080. Headline centred across the top (about 12% of height). Two columns underneath: picture left (about 55%), and on the right a vertical stack of six evenly spaced rows, each a white circular line icon plus a white label. Type covers about 25% of the frame. You read headline, then product, then the list from top to bottom. No CTA, price or separate logo.",
   "branding": "The only logo is the script wordmark on the pack. Ground colour #FC9035 matches the pack band; all type is white. A heavy slab serif (Cooper/Recoleta feel) for the headline, a rounded humanist sans for the list, one white line weight for all six icons. The consistency signal is that pack colour equals ground colour.",
   "copy": "Two-word possessive headline, 'Our Promises': warm, carrying no claim itself. Then six claims of one or two words, five built on the same 'X Free' frame (Cruelty, Sulphate, Silicone, Paraben, Alcohol) plus 'Vegan'. The claims are about what is absent; the repetition does the persuading, and the crossed-out icons look like proof.",
   "why_it_stops": "A full-bleed hot-orange tile with a juicy cut fruit is a pure colour hit in a muted feed, and six identical icon rows read as 'lots of good things' before you read a single word.",
   "jewellery_version": "'Our Promises' for Brushed Gold Huggies, 1080x1350. Saffron ground #E8A33A. One huggie is hooked over a single scored mango cube (about 2 cm) cut from a halved Alphonso cheek, and its pair lies flat beside it. The cube works as a ruler, so the scale stays honest. Soft top-left key light, the mango cropped by the bottom-left edge. The right column holds six white line icons plus labels, using only allowed truths: 18k gold tone / Surgical steel (listing-gated) / PVD finish (listing-gated) / Free insured shipping / Cash on delivery / NAIRA10. Headline 'Our Promises' in Bricolage Grotesque 800, white; logo_white at 44 px, bottom-left. OWNER DECISION: a promises column is where material benefits (water resistance, colour-fast finish) would normally sit; with logistics icons in their place the column is softer than the board intends."
  },

 "patterns": {
  "visual": "The picture makes something invisible visible. In 12 of the 19 ads the named ingredient, the scent or the inner workings becomes a physical prop or diagram (papaya, strawberries, whisky still life, lime and sea, ice, cocktail glass, exploded trimmer, knolled box). The prop world is always in the pack's own colour, so ground, product and prop are one monochrome family (9, 19, 35, 74, 100, 110). Others turn texture into a sculpture (64's S-swirl, 110's squiggle) or demonstrate use (121's strips, 97's test numbers). WHAT IS MISSING FOR NAIRA: what a jewellery feature looks like. Steel, PVD and gold tone cannot be seen the way papaya can, so Naira's feature ads need visible, nameable features: the clasp mechanism, a stone count, pave texture, the toggle, hinges, adjustability, and the shape of the piece's name (Serpentine as an S, Nocturne as night, Droplet, Woven, Prism). Name-literal props are the cheapest strong device and Naira's piece names are made for it.",
  "camera": "Mostly eye level or a three-quarter view through a normal lens. The hero fills 40-60% of the frame and is often cut off by one edge so it feels bigger than the canvas (9, 19, 31, 60). There are two lighting worlds. Dark, low-key worlds use a hard rim and specular highlights (29, 31, 48, 60, 93, 100, 116); colour-block worlds use soft high-key light or hard sun with caustics (9, 74, 104, 110). High-speed frozen motion appears in 19 and 116, and diagonals in 31, 60 and 110. Props are shot at their natural size next to the product. JEWELLERY GAP: macro photography inflates a 10 mm huggie into a dinner plate, so every Naira macro needs a known-size object in frame (a mango cube, a sugar crystal, a cowrie, a jasmine bud) or a 'true width' chip. Worn shots come only from Higgsfield at true size, with no wide-angle push like 97's.",
  "layout": "Six repeatable skeletons. (1) Product on one side, a claims column on the other (9, 19, 48, 93, 100, 116). (2) A centred hero with leader-line callouts: straight (16, 97), elbowed (29), or dashed inspection circles (60). (3) A centred hero with a bottom tab bar (35). (4) A centred texture with free-floating dot-anchored claims (64, 74). (5) A 2x3 icon grid (110). (6) Horizontal strips with a vertical spine (121). Headlines take the top 10-15%, or there is no headline at all (29, 35, 60, 64, 110). Text covers 15-45% of the frame. None of these cards has a CTA button or a price, and none has a separate logo lockup: they are PDP carousel cards. For Meta, Naira has to add a price or code chip and a small logo while keeping the claim zone clean, on the 1080x1350 kit canvas.",
  "branding": "The pack is the logo. Brand presence comes from the product label and from the ground colour lifted off the pack (#FC9035 tube band in 9, lavender cap in 19, cyan gel in 100, orange band in 110). One display face per ad, white type on colour, plus one accent colour for headings and lines (Beardo orange #D15A10, BSC tan gold #E8C89D, TMC gold). Series signals appear: BSC repeats the amber/ochre ground and the 'what's in the box' format across 16 and 103. Arata owns a typographic signature (sans with italic serif, frosted cards, monospace). For Naira, the gold tone piece cannot carry a printed logo, so the box, the wordmark and a fixed colour system (ink, bone, coral-deep, saffron, oxblood) must do the series work instead.",
  "copy": "Label headlines dominate ('Our Promises', 'Benefits', 'What you'll find in the box', 'Why you'll love it', 'Why [product]?'), followed by structured lists: icon rows, check rows, numbered timelines, top/middle/base taxonomies. The stronger lines add one device: a number up front ('90 Mins', '10%', '20%', '8 HOURS'), an antithesis ('Pink outside, powerful inside'), a repetition ('What's in the bottle is on the bottle'), an equation, a joke ('Trimmer (of course!)'), or personification ('Brings the island's juiciest gossip'). Proof is a percentage, a count or a test number. For Naira, the only numbers available are true ones (18K, listed prices in ₹, counted stones, listed sizes, ₹0 shipping), and the two strongest beauty-style claims (water resistance and a colour-fast finish) are off the table by rule. Feature copy therefore has to lean on visible mechanics, occasions and offers."
 },
 "devices": [
  "Name-literal prop pun: turn the product name into the set (Hyphen's 'Cocktail' into a margarita glass; for Naira, a Serpentine chain laid in an S, Nocturne drops on obsidian, a Toggle T-bar as a martini pick).",
  "Pack-colour monochrome world: ground, props and type accent all pulled from one product colour (orange papaya tile, pink strawberry set, cyan ice).",
  "Six-promise icon column: six identical white line icons in circles, each with a one- or two-word label, in the right 40% of the frame.",
  "'What's in the box' parts chart: a huge stacked headline at top-left, with the hero standing like a monument and leader lines naming every included item.",
  "Knolled inventory with rotated labels: items laid diagonally on a gritty colour surface, with sizes or prices set along each object's angle, and a joke as the first bullet.",
  "Exploded or cutaway view plus one giant gold number: the object pulled apart in mid-air on a dark textured ground (marked 'illustrative' for Naira).",
  "Top/Middle/Base taxonomy: three tiers as elbow-line callouts around a still life, or as three translucent tabs along the bottom edge.",
  "Equation layout: A + B = OUTCOME stacked vertically in the left column, with the result line as the biggest type in the frame.",
  "Dashed inspection circles: a dashed ring around a detail, an elbow leader line, and one claim per ring in opposite corners of an X-crossed composition.",
  "Texture sculpture with ring-dot anchors: a single glossy shape on a calm seamless, four floating claims mixing sans with an italic serif keyword, no lines.",
  "Antithesis twist headline: script 'X outside' over heavy caps 'Y INSIDE!', with pill callouts and 3D icons.",
  "Frosted-glass cards over hard raking black-and-white light: monospace caps for the facts, selective colour on the hero.",
  "Giant numeral stamp: one true number with an arched label around it ('HOURS 8 WEAR'; for Naira, 'GOLD TONE 18K').",
  "2x3 line-icon spec grid beside a diagonal product, with a squiggle line as a graphic S.",
  "'Why [product]?' question headline answered by three icon rows divided by vertical rules.",
  "Four-strip, one-face demonstration with a rotated headline spine (for Naira, one ear and four pieces, each strip generated at true size).",
  "Hand-to-viewer offer with numbers radiating to the parts they prove (Naira: counted stones and listed specs only).",
  "Honest-scale reference (missing from every winner, needed for jewellery): a known-size natural object in frame, or a 'true width [listed mm]' chip on every macro."
 ],
 "best_three": [
  19,
  31,
  64
 ]
}

 ],
 "patterns": {
  "visual": "Most of these are a packshot plus a feature list; the winners add one physical idea on top. That idea is one of: a diagram the product sits inside (125 Venn); a demonstration that lands on a number (133 drop onto a pH bar); a prop that wraps or surrounds the pack (167 peel spiral, 183 mint and camphor); a sensory surface that sets the temperature (135 condensation, 171 red velvet, 190 gel macro); a human expression (132 laugh, 195 peekaboo); or a mood world with no product at all (194). Colour-matched worlds, where ground, pack, type and icons share one hue, are near-universal (127, 135, 136, 146, 153, 190, 195). Naira's gap: a jewellery 'feature' card needs the same single physical idea (scale, gauge, wrap, exploded parts), not just a packshot with ticks.",
  "camera": "Information photography: straight-on or top-down, normal lens, soft diffuse light, deep focus. The drama comes from surfaces (slate, velvet, droplets, frosted black, gel) and colour grade, not from angles. The product is big (60–90% of frame height), usually cropped by one edge, and often tilted on a diagonal. Macro is used for texture (146, 190, 198). People are shot at eye level with an 85mm feel, chest-up, holding the product at face height true to scale with the hand. For Naira, macro and object-level shots keep scale honest; worn shots must be generated at true size.",
  "layout": "Three skeletons. (1) Split frame: a text column of about 50% on the left (headline plus 3–5 tick or check-circle bullets) and the product on the right (127, 132, 135, 153, 167, 195). (2) Centred hero with leader-line callouts ending in open circles or dots, often symmetric 2x2 (145, 172, 183, 186, 191). (3) Bands or columns repeating one structure (146 rows, 194 triptych). Headlines sit at the top over 1–2 lines. Text takes 15–50% of the frame. These are mostly PDP gallery cards, so CTA, price and offer are almost always missing; Naira paid statics should add a thin foot line (price as listed, COD, free insured shipping, code).",
  "branding": "Logos almost never appear off the pack. Brand is carried by colour world, typeface and the pack itself. Heavy geometric sans caps dominate; a serif is switched in to signal science or care (132, 136). Two-tone headlines (neutral words with an accent-coloured payoff) recur (167, 183). Consistency comes from repeating one type structure across panels or rows (146, 194). For Naira: the sage wordmark small at foot centre, and the sage box as a recurring prop to stand in for the 'pack'.",
  "copy": "Headlines run 3–8 words and almost always use a device: idiom made literal ('checks every box'), parallel pun ('XL tote / XL to-do list'), coined system name ('Triple Fusion System'), numeric antithesis ('100% / 0%'), familiarity plus news ('Your fav…, now upgraded!'), a question ('What goes inside?', 'Why so effective'), or inclusivity proved by enumeration ('All skin types', followed by the four types). Bodies are feature plus a one-line benefit with a quantifier. Proof is numbers and named components; the strongest proofs are arithmetic (198), a self-supplied benchmark (133) and dimension lines (145). Naira's equivalents are millimetres, centimetres, stone counts and listed price, all of which are verifiable."
 },
 "devices": [
  "Venn-intersection placement: the product lies in the lens where three labelled circles overlap (e.g. 'Three circles. One halo.')",
  "Idiom made literal: 'checks every box' drawn as hand-ticked checkboxes beside the product",
  "Falling object onto a graded scale: a huggie drops onto a mm bar chart, and the bar at its true size (12) is raised and lit",
  "Dimension lines with end ticks and listed measurements (50cm / 4mm / 15mm) wrapped around a product-only shot",
  "A column of rounded-rectangle zoom insets with captions, beside the full product",
  "Same-macro repetition: one piece entering from the right edge at an identical crop on stacked colour bands, one band per colourway (shade-card rhythm)",
  "A hyphen-chain tag in a filled box to compress a 3-in-1 ('STACK-WRAP-DONE')",
  "Prop wrap: the product coils around, or is coiled by, a single continuous prop (chain spiralled around a silk-wrapped cylinder)",
  "Product as billboard: the claims are printed on the product's surface; for jewellery, foil-stamped inside the box lid",
  "A countable icon row with xN counts to quantify a capsule (seven day icons: Huggies x3 · Hoops x2 · Studs x2)",
  "An exploded still life: the hero piece at the centre, echo props around it, leader lines ending in open circles",
  "2x2 symmetric callouts around a centred hero, left side right-aligned and right side left-aligned",
  "Macro texture as the entire hero, cropped by the bottom edge, with a 'shown magnified · actual Xmm' tag",
  "Parallel-pun headline built on a repeated number or size ('A 12mm huggie for your 12-hour day')",
  "A triptych of colour-graded worlds with identical type structure under each (TOP/HEART/BASE becomes STONE/SETTING/TONE)",
  "Peekaboo or monocle pose: the product covers or frames one eye (worn shot generated at true size)",
  "Numbers that add up, in dashed coupon boxes ('1 shell pearl + 2 halos = 1 stud')",
  "Numeric antithesis on offers only ('₹0 shipping. ₹0 upfront. 10% off.')",
  "Familiarity plus news headline ('Your plain gold chain, now upgraded.')",
  "Two-tone headline: neutral words in ivory or white, the payoff word in the accent colour",
  "Colour-matched world: ground, type, icons and the stone or metal all in one hue family",
  "A hand-drawn arrow from the spec list to the piece on the ear",
  "A circular magnifier inset marked 'actual size' whenever the piece is shown small at true object scale"
 ],
 "best_three": [
  133,
  125,
  194
 ]
}

 "patterns": {
  "visual": "The backbone of this group is product held at the face. In 10 of 24 ads the pack is pressed to the cheek, chin, eye, lips or shoulder (70, 88, 89, 111, 117, 126, 143, 162, 177, 179), so face and product read as one unit. Second is demonstration on skin (65, 148, 151, 170, 185, 189); third is twin frames (39, 150). The mood ads (71, 112, 118, 165, 192, 193) rely on a colour rhyme or a single human gesture. WHAT'S MISSING FOR NAIRA: none of these winners had to make a tiny product legible. Naira's version of 'pack at face' is the piece pinched between fingers at the eye or cheek, or worn in a crop tight enough (ear, collarbone, hand) that the piece is at least about 8% of frame width, plus a pointer (leader line or callout) to locate it. Full-body frames (118, 192, 193) must be cropped to shoulders-up for jewellery.",
  "camera": "Mostly eye level with a 70-100mm feel, faces cropped hard (forehead or chin cut) on a pale seamless under soft frontal beauty light. The two strongest frames break this: a low-key dark ground with a hard raking key and particles (70, 117), and identical macro twin crops (39). Outdoor light appears only in 170 and 193, and a low angle only in 185. WHAT'S MISSING FOR NAIRA: soft beauty light flattens metal and cubic zirconia. Naira needs a macro (100mm-feel) lens and a hard, directional specular key so gold tone and stones flash, on a darker or saturated kit ground (--plum, --ink, --oxblood, --saffron), not just pale seamless. Every worn frame must be generated in Higgsfield at true size.",
  "layout": "Text left, face right is the default (12 of 24: 65, 70, 126, 143, 148, 151, 162, 165, 177, 179, 189, 71). 4:5 ads use a top-centre headline over a centred subject (112, 117, 118, 150, 192, 193); 3:4 and 1:1 use a lower-third overlay band (88, 89, 111). Text runs from 10% to 60% of the frame, and the strongest ads sit at 15-30%. Almost none carry a price, offer or CTA. WHAT'S MISSING FOR NAIRA: competitors here skip the price, so Naira can own a small price/offer chip (one line, bottom-right, never a full band), plus a fixed logo corner so the series reads as one brand.",
  "branding": "Logos are almost absent. The brand lives on the pack label held to camera and in type colour pulled from the pack (indigo, forest green, magenta, brick red, maroon, teal). Heavy geometric sans caps dominate (Montserrat, Gilroy, Gotham); serif appears only in 126 and 148. Recurring consistency signals are a celebrity founder (Hyphen 88/89/185), a fixed 'MEET THE X' system (Mokobara) and a fixed two-tone headline (TMC). WHAT'S MISSING FOR NAIRA: jewellery has no label to hold up, so the brand has to come from a strict system: the Velista wordmark small in one fixed corner, one kit ground colour per product family, Bricolage or Anton caps with Cormorant italic for lead-ins, and the accent colour taken from the stone or metal (emerald, amethyst, gold-light).",
  "copy": "Five mechanics repeat. (1) Two-beat parallel lines with the second phrase tinted (65 'No greasy feel. Zero white cast.', 70, 117, 126, 170). (2) A giant number as proof (88 '17%', 162 '48 HOURS', 185 '63.3%', 189 '10%'). (3) 'Meet the X' plus a one-line use case (112, 192, 193). (4) Occasion or time-of-day framing (39, 71). (5) Self-identification lists (111). Proof comes from numbers, press strips or footnotes. WHAT'S MISSING FOR NAIRA: the beauty winners' proof is clinical or press, which Naira can't borrow. Naira's proof must be listing specs set as big type (316L, US 5-9, 18k gold tone, cubic zirconia, shell pearl, listed mm sizes) plus offer truths (free insured shipping, COD, NAIRA10, BUY2/3/4). Pull prices live as ₹[listed] at render, and feature only pieces with 20 or more in stock."
 },
 "devices": [
  "Eye occlusion: the piece is held in front of one eye (a ring with the iris seen through the band, true size); the other eye stares into the lens (from 70)",
  "Twin identical frames, one variable changed: same seed, same crop, '9 AM / 9 PM' or 'Bare + Done' labels in panels above or below (from 39, 150)",
  "Piece pinched at the cheek or chin: thumb and forefinger hold the jewellery flat to camera beside the face at the same focal depth, the jewellery version of 'pack to cheek' (from 89, 126, 162)",
  "Giant numeral lower third: a laughing close-up fades to a brand colour at the bottom 28%, with a white Anton spec such as '316L' over wide-tracked caps (from 88)",
  "Two-beat parallel headline with the second phrase tinted: 'ONE RING. / ALL EYES.' or 'THREE COILS. / ONE BANGLE.' (from 70, 117, 65)",
  "Leader line with an open-circle end, drawn from the text block to the exact earring or stud on the ear, to show where a tiny product is (from 148)",
  "Dial badge straddling a photo/colour-panel split line, carrying one listed spec ('US 5-9 ADJUSTABLE') or an offer code (from 162)",
  "Small lead-in word over a big benefit word in a left-column stack ('Catches / light', 'Also in / silver tone') (from 151)",
  "Texture halo: a macro packshot of the chain coiled in a circle behind the model's head, as background only, never composited onto skin (from 151)",
  "Costume comedy: a jeweller's headband magnifier with a wide-eyed face, plus 'The anatomy of' rows with thin line icons (from 179)",
  "Colour rhyme: wardrobe and type colour match the stone or metal (emerald blazer with Emerald Baguette Huggies; oxblood type with an oxblood satin set) (from 71, 165)",
  "Complementary colour clash: one black-and-white or gold piece as the only contrast on a flat --saffron or --sky seamless, waist-up so the piece reads (from 118)",
  "'MEET THE [PIECE]' top-centre in heavy caps with a one-line Jost sub over an over-the-shoulder or in-use frame (from 112, 192, 193)",
  "Diagonal frosted corner tabs, light top-left and dark bottom-right, with light/bold word alternation ('HELMET ON. / HUGGIES ON.') (from 170)",
  "Truth strip instead of a press strip: a grey footer band reading 'FREE INSURED SHIPPING · COD · CODE NAIRA10'; add rating or press marks only when verified (from 65)",
  "Low-key ground with suspended warm light flecks around a still face and a hard top-left specular key (from 117, 70)",
  "Self-ID pipe list in a smoky lower band: 'MADE FOR / First holes | Second holes | Stacks' (from 111)",
  "Pill tags with a one-line explainer each: 'Desk' / 'Date' (from 177)",
  "Mid-routine unglamorous frame (towel turban, sheet mask) with the piece as the one polished thing in shot (from 185, 151)",
  "Scale-honest hero packshot: an enlarged still-life only, with a 'shown larger than actual size' micro-caption and a true-size outline, beside a separately generated worn shot (from 71)"
 ],
 "best_three": [
  70,
  39,
  179
 ]
}

 "patterns": {
  "visual": "The group splits into two families. (1) Portrait plus proof (Arata ×6, Pilgrim, Re'equil ×2, Earth Rhythm): a person does something playful or blissful with the product at face height (a finger frame, a crown, a monocle, a cheek press, a spirit level), and a giant number does the proving. (2) Evidence crops (Perfora, Foxtale, Hyphen ×2, Bare Anatomy ×2, Conscious Chemist, Chemist at Play, The Man Company ×2, Minimalist): the same body part shown two or three times, locked off, so the change is the only variable. In about half the group the product is not in frame at all; the result is the hero. What Naira lacks is a visible result. For jewellery, the honest result is (a) the bare-to-worn change on one body part, (b) the process frame (a hinge, a toggle, a ring pressed to fit) and (c) macro evidence of how the piece is built.",
  "camera": "Normal lens (about 50–85mm), eye level, chest-up or tighter. Soft, diffused frontal light in almost every ad. Sets are colour matched (tile = pack = wardrobe), and in the portraits the bottom 25–30% is graded dark into the wardrobe to carry type. Evidence frames are frontal macros with identical crop and exposure, often anonymous (nose to chin, cheek, lips). The product is always on the face plane, in a hand, at true scale. Only Arata's rank 124 goes moody with hard side light, and it is the most editorial frame in the set. For Naira: generate pairs, triptychs and split faces in one Higgsfield pass at true size, so scale and light cannot drift, and never paste halves together by hand.",
  "layout": "Number-led: a giant numeral at 12–16% of frame height sits bottom-left or in a left column, a 2–4 word label sits at its cap height, and a footnote runs at 1.5–2% height. Before/after: two equal rounded panels across the middle with corner tabs, the headline top-centre and the stat beneath. Text covers 12–40% of the frame, and the top 60% of portraits stays clean. There is almost no CTA, price or logo; these are product-page secondary cards that work as ads because they look like proof, not offers. Formats: 1:1 for evidence cards and about 3:4 (1013x1350) for the Arata portraits, so Naira's 1080x1350 kit takes the Arata structure directly.",
  "branding": "Logos barely appear outside the pack. Recognition comes from a repeatable system: Arata's cream condensed numerals, rotated 'Up to', superscript footnote and tiled set; purple for Perfora, Foxtale and Bare Anatomy; teal for Pilgrim; navy on white for Re'equil. The colour lock (product = set = wardrobe = numeral colour) is the strongest consistency signal. Naira should fix one stat system (a thin condensed numeral in bone or cream, a Jost label and a footnote line, with a small logo bottom-right) and use each piece's stone colour as its set colour (emerald on sage, amethyst on plum, aqua and pink on sky).",
  "copy": "The formula is [qualifier] + [number] + [2–4 word outcome] + [footnote with source, n and duration]. The number shapes vary on purpose: a percentage, a multiplier (7.4X), a count (+28,200), a time (72-Hour, 2 WEEKS, 7 days, 1 use), a decimal (51.35%). Photos carry timeframe or quantity tags ('AFTER 7 DAYS', 'AFTER 1 USE'). Rhymes and puns are rare but the most memorable ('Purple on, Yellow gone!', ''C' the Difference'). Every number carries proof. Naira's equivalent within the house rules: listing figures (mm, cm, g, US sizes, 18K), the listed price in ₹, the codes BUY2, BUY3 and NAIRA10, and only verified review counts, with an 'as listed' footnote plus an honest plating line. OWNER DECISIONS: (a) the shower setting (rank 7) and 'Day 1 / Day 90' pairs (ranks 21 and 75) are the strongest versions of this group, but they only work with water-wear or colour-retention claims the house rule bans. Note that the Droplet Open Ring's own listing says 'Waterproof and tarnish free' and 'anti-tarnish', so the listing and the ad rule currently disagree. (b) 'Saw/Felt %' stats need a verified post-purchase survey. (c) A known face needs a signed collab."
 },
 "devices": [
  "Spec-as-stat: a giant thin condensed listing number (12mm, 40cm, 4.6g, 18K, 3X) with a 2–4 word label and an 'as listed' footnote, in the slot clinical percentages fill for beauty brands",
  "Rotated micro-qualifier: a tiny word set at 90 degrees hugging the left edge of the giant number ('just', 'every', 'only')",
  "The number swallows its unit: a giant '40', with the sentence starting 'cm of 2mm snake chain, plus a 5cm extender'",
  "Hand viewfinder: the fingers form a rhombus frame around one ear or the throat, with the piece dead centre",
  "The piece where it doesn't belong: held over an eye like a mask or monocle, or laid along the collarbone like a spirit level, generated at true size",
  "A locked-off triptych with a strange middle process frame: bare lobe, then the huggie open on its hinge through the lobe, then clicked shut",
  "A split down the middle generated as one image: half bare, half adorned, with the light warming on the 'after' half",
  "An evidence pair with a quantity tab: 'BEFORE' and 'AFTER 1 PAIR' or 'AFTER 1 CHAIN' in white corner tabs",
  "Colour lock: stone colour = wardrobe = tiled set = numeral colour",
  "A bottom gradient fading into the wardrobe carries type over a portrait, with the top 60% left clean",
  "A two-number scoreboard of different number shapes side by side (3X | 25mm), split by a hairline",
  "A footnote honesty line: '¹Size as listed. 18k gold tone plating.'",
  "Two people in two variants make a BUY2 stat bar ('01 Gold tone | 02 Silver tone')",
  "Tilted, overlapping Polaroids as an evidence pair, with a starburst sticker carrying the piece count or code",
  "Loupe report: a charcoal-and-white split layout with two 10x macro squares and white-box labels holding listing facts",
  "A timeline rail of nodes for a fit range (US 5 to US 9) or a day-to-night styling schedule",
  "Retro sticker numerals (fill, outline, drop shadow) over a warped checkerboard in the piece's colours, with a jewellery pun ('Put a bow on it.')",
  "Circular seal text built from facts ('18K GOLD TONE · CUBIC ZIRCONIA ·') wrapped around the head",
  "Gloved-hand authority: a white cotton jeweller's glove holds the piece upright on high-key white beside decimal listing figures (6.6mm, 4.6g)"
 ],
 "best_three": [
  2,
  20,
  21
 ]
}

 "patterns": {
  "visual": "Four image families. (1) The result face with no product: eyes closed, laughing or bliss, hand on face (14, 45, 61, 105, 184). (2) The product hero surrounded by a chorus of cards or stats (13, 81, 98, 156, 168, 140). (3) Texture or skin as the canvas: cream swirl, jelly wave, back, décolletage, lips in macro (23, 44, 106, 141, 154). (4) Documentary realism: a group of ordinary testers, or an unretouched older woman (12, 84). In beauty, the feeling stands in for the product. For jewellery the piece must stay in frame, worn at true size, because it is the result. The 'result face' becomes 'the face plus the piece', and the texture macro becomes the metal or stone surface.",
  "camera": "Mostly eye level, 50-85mm beauty portraits under soft frontal or wrap light, high-key, with dewy highlights. Models are cut out and bleed off one side edge (usually the right) so the other side is free for type. Hands enter from a frame edge holding the product at the jaw or cheek (13, 18, 23, 81, 184). Macro texture shots use raking light (106, 44). Colour echo is planned in camera: lips, nails and wardrobe match the stat colour (18, 61, 184, 23). Product scale stays honest because packs are held normally. For Naira, that means Higgsfield-generated worn shots at listed sizes only.",
  "layout": "A text column on the left 40-50% and the image on the right, mirrored left to right across a carousel (105 against 14/45). Numerals at 150-220px are the visual anchor, 2-4 stats stacked. A two-tone horizontal colour block splits the frame (14, 45, 105). Reviews sit in 3 cards zig-zagging or stepping diagonally along the product's angle (13, 81, 156). Every stat ad has a footnote strip in the bottom 2-3%. Text covers 20-55% of the frame. There is almost never a CTA, and almost never an on-canvas logo. Naira's canvas is 4:5 (1080x1350), so the extra height goes to the image, not more stats.",
  "branding": "Logos are nearly absent: the brand is carried by one accent colour pulled from the pack and used for numerals, stars and rules (Foxtale orange, Sugar cyan, Conscious Chemist red or teal, Bare Anatomy green), and by a repeatable template (Dr. Sheth's condensed caps, Montserrat stats, colour block, plus-sign motif, eyes-closed model). Typical pairing: condensed poster caps or heavy geometric sans for headers and numbers, with a light humanist sans for body. Naira's equivalent: one accent per ad from the kit (coral-deep, oxblood, sage-deep, saffron, gold #C9A55C), Anton or Bricolage for numerals, Jost for body, and the Velista wordmark small or omitted.",
  "copy": "Number first, with a hedged perception verb ('users claim*', 'agreed', 'reported') and an openly small sample in the footnote (15, 30, 35, 40, 50, 300 people, over 2-4 weeks). Headlines are either labels ('THE PROOF', 'Consumer Study Results', 'What users experienced') or tricolons ('Tested. Loved. Trusted.', 'Reviewed, Rated & Loved'). Reviews carry a first name and city, with grammar, emojis and elongations left raw. Lower numbers (70%, 72%, 80%) are kept in for believability. What Naira is missing: the fuel of this whole genre. The repo audit found no review app installed and the caption rules forbid ratings, order counts and reviews, so every % and every quote here is unavailable. The honest substitute is the spec stat (mm, cm, g, ₹ as listed) set in the same giant-numeral treatment, with the footnote used for truth ('*Sizes as listed. 18k gold tone plating.'). Prices are taken from the repo's SKU library and need checking against the live listing before export."
 },
 "devices": [
  "Spec-stat swap: replace the giant user % with a listed measurement in the same 180-220px numeral weight (25mm, 12g, 50cm, 0 stones, ₹1,449), with the explanation as body underneath.",
  "Footnote of honesty: a 20px strip in the bottom 3% stating the source of every number ('*Sizes as listed. 18k gold tone plating. ₹ as listed'). It copies the winners' credibility device without inventing a study.",
  "Colour-echo triangle: the numeral colour = the lip and nail colour = the wardrobe (oxblood, coral-deep or sage-deep from the kit), so number and face read as one statement (from 18, 61, 184, 23).",
  "Label headline: a two-word section label in condensed caps ('THE DETAILS', 'AS LISTED', 'WHAT YOU GET') in place of the beauty brands' 'THE PROOF', which Naira cannot back without data.",
  "Staccato tricolon: three one-word or one-figure sentences with full stops ('BRAIDED. GOLD TONE. 25MM.', 'Measured, Weighed & Boxed.').",
  "Number-first anaphora: three lines that all start with a figure ('4g on the finger. / 2 stones, 2 unmatched bezels. / ₹2,049, free insured shipping.').",
  "Pierce the headline: the piece or the hand physically breaks between two headline lines set in contrasting styles (upright ink, italic accent), from 81.",
  "Swatch in hand: product and result in the same hand. For Naira, the hand holding the open box also wears the ring at true size.",
  "Body as page: type set straight onto bare collarbones or a shoulder blade beside the worn chain, never over the piece (141, 154).",
  "Texture-wall macro: an extreme raking-light macro of the metal or stone surface fills 60% of the frame like sculpture, with giant gold #C9A55C numerals in the empty side (106, 44).",
  "Hoop gauge: the donut infographic becomes the hoop itself, with rings drawn at relative true diameters (12mm against 25mm) and the packshot nested inside (98).",
  "Ruled spec table: a 4-5 row two-column hairline table (Inner diameter | 17mm ...) beside a worn portrait (184).",
  "Seal of truths: a circular rubber-stamp seal carrying verifiable service facts ('FREE INSURED SHIPPING · CASH ON DELIVERY'), never a lab, test or rating seal (61, 13).",
  "Chorus of cards: three white offset-shadow cards zig-zag around a hand-held hero, carrying facts now and genuine verified reviews later (13, 81, 156).",
  "Lab-notebook page: millimetre graph-paper ground, monospace eyebrow, a hairline-split two-column stat row and superscript footnotes over a desaturated documentary portrait (84).",
  "Mirror template carousel: one fixed system (colour block, condensed header, stat stack, eyes-closed model) flipped left and right and recoloured per collection, so the brand shows without a logo (14/45/105).",
  "Jury line-up: five ordinary women, five different in-stock earrings, each pointing at her own lobe. It must never be captioned as testers or customers unless that is true (12).",
  "OWNER DECISION 1, proof infrastructure: install a review app and collect verified post-purchase reviews and counts. That unlocks the % stat, the named review card and the 'loved by N' seal, the core of this whole group, which Naira cannot honestly run today.",
  "OWNER DECISION 2, banned claims: ranks 44, 45 and 14 would be much stronger with the waterproof / tarnish-free claim the board suggested (for example a '%' after a 30-day wear panel, or a shower demo). The house rules forbid it; it would need a substantiated wear test and the owner's explicit sign-off before any wording changes."
 ],
 "best_three": [
  81,
  18,
  141
 ]
}

 "patterns": {
  "visual": "Bundles appear as one family in one colour world: tonal peach, mauve, red on lilac, black and tan, gold on cream. One container or stage holds the set: a pouch, jewel case, gift box, podium or shelf. Comparisons always put the brand in colour and the rival in grey, generic and unbranded (the greyed dropper, the grey columns, the empty pills), or they show the difference on one body (the split face, the twin arches). The gap for Naira: every product on this board is large and carries its own logo, while jewellery is small and has none. Each Naira version therefore needs three things: (a) a macro or tight crop so the pieces read at thumbnail size; (b) the brand carried by the shipped box, the Velista wordmark or a colour field; (c) a container prop that matches what Naira actually ships.",
  "camera": "Two families. Bundles are shot straight on at eye level or as a 90-degree flat lay, with a normal lens, deep focus and true relative scale. Within that: Sugar's festive look is a low three-quarter view in hard warm sun through blinds, with a marble plinth, gold chain prop and faceted backdrop; BSC uses flat 2D cut-outs with no shadows on a saturated ground; Conscious Chemist uses top-down with hard window mullion shadows. Comparisons either have no camera at all (vector tables) or use a macro of a face or eye framed identically on both sides. For Naira: generate worn shots directly at true size; keep packshot groups at true relative scale; when a piece has to be enlarged, give it its own tile with its listed size.",
  "layout": "The headline sits in the top 15-30% and the products in the lower 60%. The bottom strip is either left empty (4:5, clear of Meta's overlay) or used as a 3-column info band. Labels sit on the product they name, not in a separate list: staggered step labels, dotted arrows, hand-drawn arrows, frosted cards, catalogue callouts. Comparisons use a 50/50 split with a VS disc in the seam and mirrored rows, or podium height, with the brand column first and in colour. None of the 24 puts a price on the canvas, and only rank 5 states an offer; the offer is carried by a named pack ('Edit', 'Pack', 'Trio', '3-step'). Naira has things to say here (BUY2/3/4, listed prices, COD, free insured shipping), so one small chip should carry them without crowding the image.",
  "branding": "The logo is rarely on the canvas. It lives on the packs, a deboss, a clasp plate or a column header, while the brand colour floods the ground or the winning column (Foxtale magenta, Arata red, BSC blue, Plum orange, Bella Vita red). Series consistency comes from a repeated system: Sugar uses glitter italic serif, bold caps, a faceted backdrop and sun stripes across ranks 5, 62 and 80. Naira has to place the Velista wordmark on the canvas or on the shipped box, and fix one repeatable system per series from kit.css: Cormorant italic with Jost caps on bone, with warm blind stripes, for festive work; Anton on a loud ground (terracotta, saffron, plum, sky) for offers; sage-deep as the colour of the winning column in comparisons.",
  "copy": "Counts and named systems: '3-STEP ROUTINE', 'The smoothing trio', 'Life Ready Pack', 'The Dhanteras Glow Edit', 'four iconic perfumes'. Devices: antithesis ('A Little Gold. A Lot of Glow.'), anaphora ('One in the...' four times), an equation ('5+5 = ...'), personification ('Your Festive Plus One'), an object pun ('One Book. Your Whole Look.'), Hinglish ('DE-TAN HAR ROZ'), and self-selection ('Pick...', 'Find your match', 'Know your...', 'Choose the right...'). Comparisons rest on numbers (40%, 12%, 1.6X, 86%) or mirrored X/tick rows. Naira may not invent numbers. Use only listed figures (18K, ₹ prices, piece counts, BUY codes) and listed materials (surgical stainless steel and PVD where the listing says so, cubic zirconia where listed, adjustable where listed). Prices here were read from Shopify on 4 Nov 2026; read them again before rendering. Owner decision: ranks 8, 30, 37, 38, 73, 102 and 158 would be much stronger with a water-wear or colour-hold row. Some live listing Care texts already make that claim, but the ad rules forbid it until the owner decides and a documented wear test exists."
 },
 "devices": [
  "Anaphora occasion map for multi-buys: 'One for the office / One for the airport / One for the date / One for the sangeet', one caption per tilted packshot, to sell BUY4 (rank 25)",
  "Equation headline that the viewer finishes: 'NECK + EARS + WRIST + FINGER = DONE.' over a fan of packshots (rank 58)",
  "Step staircase: 'STEP 1 / 2 / 3' labels set at staggered heights directly above each piece on graded plinths, in one tonal colour world (ranks 59, 139)",
  "Frosted glass cards aligned above each product column, with a mono caps verb and a 'with [piece] for [use]' line (rank 63)",
  "Bottom info band in 3 columns with sun and moon usage icons, aligned to the pieces above (rank 159)",
  "Two-tier hand labels (evocative name in colour over plain name and price) with dotted looping arrows: the best fix for jewellery's small size (rank 49)",
  "Container spill: the box Naira ships in, lid off, with the set fanning out of it, shot top-down in hard window light with mullion shadow bars (ranks 43, 90)",
  "Festive luxury light: hard warm sun through blinds raking across a marble plinth, with a gold chain prop and a faceted or plaster backdrop (ranks 62, 80)",
  "Prism streak: one rainbow light band across a pale silver backdrop, generated as light in the scene, never painted onto stones (rank 90)",
  "Mixed-type headline stack: small caps, then a huge italic serif occasion word, then bold caps ('YOUR Sangeet PLUS ONE') (ranks 62, 80)",
  "Named edit plus offer line: 'The [Occasion] Edit' + '[count] pieces · code BUYn · free insured shipping · COD' (rank 5)",
  "Catalogue callouts with italic serif numerals: '01 TOGGLE LINK CHAIN ₹1,199' set beside each piece (rank 80)",
  "Two-step podium in brand colour, where height means rank: Naira piece on top, unbranded generic below (rank 37)",
  "Twin arches: the same macro framing and light on both sides, the problem on the left and the Naira fix on the right (rank 38)",
  "One face split by a hairline: a single generated portrait with one ear bare and one wearing the piece, so nothing is composited (rank 92)",
  "Brand-colour column against grey rival columns, with cross-section or gauge icons (rank 8)",
  "Empty outline pill against filled tick toggle, where empty means 'not stated' rather than 'no' (rank 102)",
  "Colour-split panels with a VS disc in the seam and mirrored X/tick rows (ranks 30, 158)",
  "Big listed figures as proof ('18K', '₹1,199', '₹0 insured shipping') beside a desaturated generic rival, with a footnote citing the listing (rank 73)",
  "Self-selection grid of three faces, worn at true size and generated directly, each with its own packshot tile underneath ('Pick a huggie for your vibe') (rank 36)",
  "'Find your match' split with the listed price as the giant numeral and a WHO IT'S FOR row (rank 51)",
  "Occasion grid 'PERFECT TO WEAR': piece name over an occasion bar, 2x2, above a glowing stage (rank 161)",
  "Two-colour Hinglish headline with hand-drawn arrows to role labels: 'SHINE HAR ROZ', 'Office wali / Chai-break wali / Date-night wali' (rank 152)",
  "Service seal in place of invented proof: a round badge reading 'COD · FREE INSURED SHIPPING' where beauty brands put 'clinically tested' (rank 159)"
 ],

   "visual_idea": "The 'shh' gesture. An index finger presses against glossy red lips while the same hand holds the bottle at the chin, so the perfume reads as a secret kept at the mouth. The red nails repeat the lip colour, which welds hand, lips and product into one shape.",
   "camera": "Tight beauty close-up with a short tele feel (about 90-100mm), shot from just below the chin and looking up. The face is cut at the nostrils, so there are no eyes.    "layout": "1:1. The only text is a two-line headline set flush left in the left third, at 21-30% of the height, covering about 8% of the frame. The eye goes lips (top-right), then headline (left), then bottle (centre). There is no logo lockup, CTA or price, and the left half is open white.",
   "branding": "The RENEE wordmark appears only on the bottle label. The type is a geometric sans in caps (Montserrat-like), black on white, with line 1 in Light and line 2 in Bold.    "copy": "'LUXURY THAT / STAYS WITH YOU', 5 words. Weight splits it into a light set-up and a bold payoff. It turns a longevity benefit into a relationship line with a double meaning: the scent lingers, and so does the moment. There is no proof.",

 "patterns": {
  "visual": "One idea per frame, and nothing else. The strongest ads put the product in another object's role or world: a sundae (47), a sling bag (72), a hazard (76), a pirate's bottle (85), an heirloom medallion (94), a counter to lean on (91). The next tier shows a body fragment doing a gesture: lips with a finger (6), a torso (72), a forearm ladder (114), pouring hands (131), an eye covered by the product (83). Frames live in a monochrome colour world taken from the pack: pink (47), orange (76), raspberry (131), teal (83), copper (72), black and white plus teal (85). Plain range lineups (173, 176, 200) are the weakest, and the only one that survives is the one that adds an idea, the wolf shadow (174).",
  "camera": "Two lighting camps. Soft high-key light on white or grey for intimacy and education (6, 50, 114, 129, 200). Hard directional sun with patterned shadows for mood (72 slats, 83 light shaft, 91 crisp floor shadows, 85 side light). Renders sit on glossy reflective floors with a glow behind the product (47, 76, 173, 174, 176), and the hero takes 40-65% of the frame width. In people shots the face is cut off (6, 72, 114, 131) or covered (83), so the product is the only focal point and is held at the body at plausible size. Depth of field is shallow everywhere a person appears.",
  "layout": "12 of 16 are 1:1, two are 5:6 and one is 3:4, so none is built for 4:5 safe zones. The text is a single block in one corner or a top band, at most three lines, covering 8-35% of the frame. A two-weight set-up/payoff headline (light line, bold line) appears in at least 8 of 16. Split-panel layouts (114's 50/50, 50's two bands, 47's 45/55) carry the information ads. Across all 16 there is no price, and no CTA button. Only one ad carries an offer (176), and only one carries a product name as the headline (47). For Naira, this group shows what to borrow (one idea, a corner headline) and what to add: the price chip, code and wordmark these brands skip.",
  "branding": "These brands rely on the pack to brand the ad: the logo sits on the bottle, compact or suitcase, and the canvas almost never has a lockup (only 47 repeats it). Jewellery has no pack, so Naira must place the Velista wordmark on the canvas every time; Foxtale (50) shows the cost of skipping it, an unbranded ad. Pack colour sets the ground and the headline accent colour (47, 76, 85, 131, 83). The headline is set in the pack's own typeface (47). Typefaces: geometric sans caps for posters (6, 85, 91, 173, 174, 200), condensed grotesk for numbers and steps (114, 83), serif for heritage, festive and trust (94, 72, 129).",
  "copy": "4-7 words. The devices are a pun (47 'Favourite Scoop'), a metaphor (72 'plus one', 85 'ocean at your fingertips'), a double meaning (6 'stays with you'), a warning format (76), a number (114 '6', 129 '2-Year'), a diagnostic question (50), an introduction (200 'Meet the') and a campaign statement (91 'The new face of'). Claims are emotional, not technical. Proof is almost absent: only a count (114), a warranty (129) and materials (94). Naira can win where they are thin, by pairing one witty line with one verifiable fact (the live price, 18k gold tone on stainless steel per the listing, BUY2/3/4, free insured shipping, COD)."
 },
 "devices": [
  "'Shh' crop: index finger on the lips with the piece at the lip line, face cut at the nose, light/bold two-line headline in the empty left third, and the secret paid off by the price",
  "Product-as-dessert: pieces perched on colour-matched scoops in small glass coupes, a thin syrup or honey drip as the vertical eye-line, flavour count tied to the BUY3 code",
  "Product in costume: the piece worn as the outfit's 'plus one' in a festive crop, chin to waist, under low sun with jaali or slatted shadows",
  "Caution-tape X: two neon tapes crossing behind a macro packshot, the warning line written as a compliment ('⚠ CAUSES STARING'), on a pack-free loud ground (terracotta + pista lime), with grain",
  "Selective colour: a black-and-white generated portrait in which only the stones or metal and one headline word stay in colour",
  "Built colour-block set (chai tapri, bus stop) in hard sun, a person leaning on it, cropped waist-up so the jewellery stays readable",
  "Labelled lineup: range shot on stepped black-marble plinths, each piece tagged with an hour or occasion, with the BUY4 code",
  "Shadow reveal: a single hard spotlight so a small piece casts a symbolic shadow (crown, key) on a plaster wall",
  "Two-tier decoder: wrist-vein swatches, then gold tone, silver tone or both, ending in 'Neutral? wear both: BUY2'",
  "Monocle how-to: the piece held over one eye, the other eye to camera, three dot-anchored callouts orbiting the head in a moody tiled room",
  "Body ruler: a labelled ladder on rules pointing at each piece on a wrist or ear, next to a giant numeral panel ('3 PIECES. ONE STACK.')",
  "Liquid pour: a snake chain falling hand to hand like syrup, frozen, on a monochrome square-tile wall, with HOW TO WEAR / PAIRS WITH label bars",
  "Heirloom invitation: double-rule cream border, the piece in a medallion on a coloured disc, a spaced serif title, a one-line meaning, two material badges in the bottom corners",
  "Candour card: 'What it is / What it isn't' bullets with a scalloped COD seal",
  "Lit stage: black mirror floor inside a warm LED jharokha arch, an angled ribbon carrying the live code",
  "Two-weight headline: a light set-up line over a bold payoff line, always in one corner block, never more than three lines",
  "Accent word in the stone's colour (sampled from the photo)",
  "Owner decision (rule conflict): the caution tape (76), the bathroom pour (131) and the trust card (129) would each be strongest with a water or tarnish claim. The house rule forbids it, so these were designed without it"
